It’s more that my eating’s been all over the place for months—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up buying sweets in the bakery aisle, even though I don’t really enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Not really like… a huge amount all at once, I don’t think. It’s more that my eating’s just been kind of turned around for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I know that’s probably not quite what you mean by “can’t stop,” but that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not usually. I mean, sometimes I feel a little silly afterward, like, “Why did I buy that?” but I wouldn’t say it’s strong guilt. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or body shape when you decide what to eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not in a planned way. I’m not really thinking, like, about my shape when I choose food. It’s more just whatever I can manage or whatever seems easy at the time. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is influenced by how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m feeling low or sort of worn out, my eating just gets all turned around. Some days I’ll only manage toast and coffee, and other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… yes, I guess my mood does affect it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you're just not interested in eating or food in general?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I don’t mean I never want food at all, but for months now it’s been kind of all over the place. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I guess that does mean I’m not really interested in eating in a normal way… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight, such as having to buy smaller clothes or having people comment on how much thinner you look?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of my weight, honestly… I don’t think I’ve been buying smaller clothes or anything like that. It’s more what I came in about, that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’m not sure if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual weight loss… I haven’t been weighing myself. It’s more that, for months now, my eating has just been kind of off. Like some days I’ll only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly… sorry, is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your unusual eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still manage the basics for my aunt, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything. But I have missed some church planning calls, and I’ve put off fair projects I normally would’ve handled. Sometimes I have to ask neighbors to cover things that used to be easy for me. It’s just made caregiving and staying involved in town feel harder than it used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy eating more if you were feeling better emotionally?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… I mean, that’s why I brought it up. My eating’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’d like to think if I felt more like myself, eating would feel more normal again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in eating on some days is directly related to your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least mostly. I don’t want to make too much of it, but my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It feels tied to how I’m doing inside, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I haven’t been keeping close track, so I don’t want to overstate it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue or weakness since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when I’ve had a full night—like if my aunt doesn’t call out for me—I can still feel worn down by breakfast. Little things, like carrying laundry or making phone calls, take more out of me than they should. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel weak or tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but even if I sleep through the night and my aunt doesn’t need me, I can feel worn out by breakfast. Things like carrying laundry or even making a few phone calls take more out of me than they probably should. My eating’s just gotten all turned around these past months—some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s not something I’m proud of, but… is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you're eating or thinking about food?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not happy with how I eat sometimes, but I can’t say I’m sitting there worrying I’ll gain weight. It’s more just… I don’t know, the food habits feel off. I’m not really thinking, like, about my shape when I choose food. It’s more just whatever I can manage or whatever seems easy at the time. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is influenced by how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m feeling low or sort of worn out, my eating just gets all turned around. Some days I’ll only manage toast and coffee, and other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… yes, I guess my mood does affect it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you're just not interested in eating or food in general?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I don’t mean I never want food at all, but for months now it’s been kind of all over the place. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I guess that does mean I’m not really interested in eating in a normal way… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight, such as having to buy smaller clothes or having people comment on how much thinner you look?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of my weight, honestly… I don’t think I’ve been buying smaller clothes or anything like that. It’s more what I came in about, that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’m not sure if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual weight loss… I haven’t been weighing myself. It’s more that, for months now, my eating has just been kind of off. Like some days I’ll only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly… sorry, is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your unusual eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still manage the basics for my aunt, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything. But I have missed some church planning calls, and I’ve put off fair projects I normally would’ve handled. Sometimes I have to ask neighbors to cover things that used to be easy for me. It’s just made caregiving and staying involved in town feel harder than it used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy eating more if you were feeling better emotionally?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… I mean, that’s why I brought it up. My eating’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’d like to think if I felt more like myself, eating would feel more normal again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in eating on some days is directly related to your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least mostly. I don’t want to make too much of it, but my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels tied to how I’m doing inside, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I haven’t been keeping close track, so I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that my eating has been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets at the bakery even though I don’t really enjoy them. So my clothes maybe feel different here and there, but I’m not sure I could say exactly how much. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue or weakness since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when I’ve had a full night—like if my aunt doesn’t call out for me—I can still feel worn down by breakfast. Little things, like carrying laundry or making phone calls, take more out of me than they should. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel weak or tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but even if I sleep through the night and my aunt doesn’t need me, I can feel worn out by breakfast. Things like carrying laundry or even making a few phone calls take more out of me than they probably should. Is that what you mean by weak or tired?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating just to have something to do, rather than because you're actually hungry?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, some days it feels like that. I don’t know if I’d call it hunger exactly. My eating’s just gotten all turned around these past months—some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s not something I’m proud of, but… is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you're eating or thinking about food?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not happy with how I eat sometimes, but I can’t say I’m sitting there worrying I’ll gain weight. It’s more just… I don’t know, the food habits feel off. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat or think about eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight. I know I already said something like that, but it really isn’t what’s on my mind when I eat. If anything, I just feel a little embarrassed that my eating is so uneven, but not scared about weight, exactly. I’m not really thinking, like, about my shape when I choose food. It’s more just whatever I can manage or whatever seems easy at the time. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is influenced by how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m feeling low or sort of worn out, my eating just gets all turned around. Some days I’ll only manage toast and coffee, and other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… yes, I guess my mood does affect it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you're just not interested in eating or food in general?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I don’t mean I never want food at all, but for months now it’s been kind of all over the place. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I guess that does mean I’m not really interested in eating in a normal way… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight, such as having to buy smaller clothes or having people comment on how much thinner you look?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of my weight, honestly… I don’t think I’ve been buying smaller clothes or anything like that. It’s more what I came in about, that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’m not sure if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual weight loss… I haven’t been weighing myself. It’s more that, for months now, my eating has just been kind of off. Like some days I’ll only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly… sorry, is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your unusual eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still manage the basics for my aunt, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything. But I have missed some church planning calls, and I’ve put off fair projects I normally would’ve handled. Sometimes I have to ask neighbors to cover things that used to be easy for me. It’s just made caregiving and staying involved in town feel harder than it used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy eating more if you were feeling better emotionally?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… I mean, that’s why I brought it up. My eating’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’d like to think if I felt more like myself, eating would feel more normal again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in eating on some days is directly related to your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least mostly. I don’t want to make too much of it, but my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels tied to how I’m doing inside, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I haven’t been keeping close track, so I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that my eating has been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets at the bakery even though I don’t really enjoy them. So my clothes maybe feel different here and there, but I’m not sure I could say exactly how much. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue or weakness since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when I’ve had a full night—like if my aunt doesn’t call out for me—I can still feel worn down by breakfast. Little things, like carrying laundry or making phone calls, take more out of me than they should. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel weak or tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but even if I sleep through the night and my aunt doesn’t need me, I can feel worn out by breakfast. Things like carrying laundry or even making a few phone calls take more out of me than they probably should. Is that what you mean by weak or tired?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating just to have something to do, rather than because you're actually hungry?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, some days it feels like that. I don’t know if I’d call it hunger exactly. My eating’s just gotten all turned around these past months—some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s not something I’m proud of, but… is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you're eating or thinking about food?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I know that’s probably not quite what you mean by “can’t stop,” but that’s what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not usually. I mean, sometimes I feel a little silly afterward, like, “Why did I buy that?” but I wouldn’t say it’s strong guilt. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or body shape when you decide what to eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no… not in a planned way. I’m not really thinking, like, about my shape when I choose food. It’s more just whatever I can manage or whatever seems easy at the time. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is influenced by how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m feeling low or sort of worn out, my eating just gets all turned around. Some days I’ll only manage toast and coffee, and other days I’ll find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… yes, I guess my mood does affect it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you're just not interested in eating or food in general?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I don’t mean I never want food at all, but for months now it’s been kind of all over the place. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’ll end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. I guess that does mean I’m not really interested in eating in a normal way… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight, such as having to buy smaller clothes or having people comment on how much thinner you look?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really kept track of my weight, honestly… I don’t think I’ve been buying smaller clothes or anything like that. It’s more what I came in about, that my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’m not sure if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual weight loss… I haven’t been weighing myself. It’s more that, for months now, my eating has just been kind of off. Like some days I’ll only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight exactly… sorry, is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your unusual eating pattern been interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still manage the basics for my aunt, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything. But I have missed some church planning calls, and I’ve put off fair projects I normally would’ve handled. Sometimes I have to ask neighbors to cover things that used to be easy for me. It’s just made caregiving and staying involved in town feel harder than it used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy eating more if you were feeling better emotionally?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… I mean, that’s why I brought it up. My eating’s been all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. So… I’d like to think if I felt more like myself, eating would feel more normal again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in eating on some days is directly related to your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, at least mostly. I don’t want to make too much of it, but my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It feels tied to how I’m doing inside, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I haven’t been keeping close track, so I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more that my eating has been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m buying sweets at the bakery even though I don’t really enjoy them. So my clothes maybe feel different here and there, but I’m not sure I could say exactly how much. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like fatigue or weakness since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even when I’ve had a full night—like if my aunt doesn’t call out for me—I can still feel worn down by breakfast. Little things, like carrying laundry or making phone calls, take more out of me than they should. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel weak or tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… I think so. I don’t want to sound like I’m complaining, but even if I sleep through the night and my aunt doesn’t need me, I can feel worn out by breakfast. Things like carrying laundry or even making a few phone calls take more out of me than they probably should. Is that what you mean by weak or tired?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating just to have something to do, rather than because you're actually hungry?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, some days it feels like that. I don’t know if I’d call it hunger exactly. My eating’s just gotten all turned around these past months—some days I only pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s not something I’m proud of, but… is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you're eating or thinking about food?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not happy with how I eat sometimes, but I can’t say I’m sitting there worrying I’ll gain weight. It’s more just… I don’t know, the food habits feel off. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight when you eat or think about eating?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight. I know I already said something like that, but it really isn’t what’s on my mind when I eat. If anything, I just feel a little embarrassed that my eating is so uneven, but not scared about weight, exactly. Is that clear?\n\nDoctor: Are you eating in a way that's intended to prevent weight gain?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m doing anything on purpose to keep from gaining weight. It’s more that my eating has just gotten… kind of all turned around for months.
